#7427. Senior Architect, Enterprise Agentic Automation at Camunda

Camunda is the enterprise platform for agentic orchestration, enabling organizations to coordinate AI agents, people, and systems across complex, end-to-end business processes. With built-in governance, auditability, and human oversight, Camunda gives enterprises the control they need to move AI from pilots to production — safely and at scale. Trusted by over 700 organizations worldwide, including 9 of top 10 US banks, Camunda helps enterprises boost operational efficiency, accelerate time-to-value, and deliver better customer experiences.Fully remote and global, we are in the middle of something bigger: transforming into an AI-first organisation, built on our own platform. We use Agentic AI to automate, orchestrate intelligent processes, and elevate human contribution across every team.Named GP Bullhound’s Top 100 Next Unicorn list, 2025 Great Place to Work certified. Visionary in 2025 Gartner® Magic Quadrant™ for Business Orchestration and Automation Technologies. ranked 3rd in Flexa's 2026 Most Flexible Companies, We’re growing fast and looking for top talent to join our team. If you want meaningful work, visible impact and put something genuinely rare on your CV, keep reading.

We run Camunda on Camunda. Our Enterprise Agentic Automation Team (EAAT) builds production-grade agentic orchestration using Camunda's platform for Camunda's own operations, from Finance to Support to Go-to-Market. As Senior Architect, you own delivery on our EAAT-led initiatives end to end. You partner with process owners to uncover where the biggest value lies, redesign how the work should flow, decide where agents, rules or people handle each step, and shape it into scope our engineers can build from directly. Then you lead technical delivery management with our engineers, steering increments, dependencies and decisions until you get it live and prove the value. You won't design and hand over: you stay until it works in production, and keep evolving it.

Own delivery of our use cases end to end, business and technical, from discovery to production and value realization

Run discovery with process owners: as-is baseline, target process, and where agents, rules or humans handle each step

Turn target processes into epics engineers can build from directly: scenarios, acceptance criteria, increments

Design solutions within our reference architecture, and bring new patterns based on lessons learned

Lead technical delivery on your initiatives: plan increments with the engineers, manage dependencies and risks, and keep process owners and sponsors aligned on progress and decisions

Define and track value per initiative and increment, and keep business decisions moving

Feed delivery learnings back into the reference architecture, our delivery method and Camunda's product

Ability and/or willingness to use our product.

7+ years in solution architecture or technical/process consulting, with an engineering background (Java, JavaScript or similar) and significant time delivering for the business or customers

A track record of leading process orchestration initiatives (Camunda or comparable) from discovery to production, not just designing them and handing over

Strong business analysis and process management skills: runs discovery and process redesign workshops with business stakeholders, models processes in BPMN, and turns the outcome into solid solution designs

Solution and data architecture in an enterprise context: integrations and APIs, and the data a process and its agents need across systems

An AI-first mindset, including first experience designing and building enterprise-grade agentic solutions

Holds their own with senior stakeholders and gets decisions made

Experience in Finance or other back-office process domains and operations (P2P, Q2C)

Experience building value cases from operational data (baselines, volumes, value per increment), value engineering or benefits tracking

Experience with process intelligence or mining tools

Working knowledge of enterprise systems such as NetSuite, Salesforce or Zip

We offer competitive, fair, and transparent compensation. Salary ranges are location-based, with Standard and Major markets (global tech hubs) reflecting local competition.

The Annual Total Target Cash (base salary + 100% variable target, where applicable) shown below spans from the minimum in a Standard market to the maximum in a Major market. Final offers depend on skills, experience, and location, and we typically hire in the first half of the range to allow room for growth:

United States: $171,100.00 to $275,800.00

United Kingdom: £107,400.00 to £176,700.00

If you’re based elsewhere, you’ll be hired via Remote.com (our global employer partner), and your Talent Acquisition Partner will provide a personalized Total Rewards Calculator after your first interview.

Equity: We also offer equity (where applicable) through our Virtual Stock Option Plan (VSOP).

We invest in your wellbeing, growth, and ability to connect, along with perks that support you no matter where you’re based. Our benefits are globally designed and locally delivered where applicable.

Remote & Flexible: Work from anywhere with the setup that suits you, home office budget, co-working space support, and flexible time off to recharge when you need it.

In Person Connection: We invest in meaningful face time through our Annual Kickoff (Vienna in 2025, Madrid in 2026!), team offsites, and Camundi Connection Budgets, including contributing to meetups while travelling,, and local gatherings with fellow Camundi.

Health & Wellbeing: Access locally tailored healthcare, Modern Health for global mental wellbeing, and our Live Well Lifestyle Spending Account (LSA), a flexible, global benefit that puts you in control of your whole life, not just work, from: staying active, to caring for family, exploring personal passions, meaningful experiences, and investing in your financial wellbeing. Financial Security: Retirement and pension plans (often with company contributions), plus life and disability insurance where relevant.

Professional Growth: Up to $/€/£1,000 per year for self-driven learning: courses, certifications, books, you decide!

AI in our hiring process: Camunda may use AI tools to aid the screening of applications and during the interview process. You can learn more here

”Everyone is welcome at Camunda” — it’s a celebrated component of our culture. We strive to create an inclusive environment that empowers our people. At Camunda, we honour diverse cultures and backgrounds and are proud to be an equal opportunity employer. All qualified applicants will receive consideration without regard to gender, race, ethnicity, religion, belief, sexual orientation, age, disability or any other protected characteristics under applicable law. We are looking forward to your application!

Come join us and be part of Camunda’s incredible journey: Make an impact at a pivotal moment in our story!

Design at Ledgy is AI-first. Work starts with AI models, moves into working prototypes in our codebase, and lands in Figma for the system. Every team ships agent features. You'll make sure the whole team does this well, consistently, and with judgement.

#7422. When It Comes to GTA 6, Every Brand Suddenly Wants to Break the Rules

It’s funny how quickly brand safety becomes negotiable when the entire world is paying attention.

For decades, Grand Theft Auto has been everything advertisers avoided: violence, crime, sex, drugs, and an unapologetic disregard for authority. The franchise fueled gaming’s biggest moral controversies.

Now GTA 6 has arrived, and brands can’t get close enough.

Dozens of brands lined up to associate themselves with the release on Thursday (Nov. 19), projected to be the largest entertainment launch in history with estimated sales topping $2.3 billion.

From Popeyes creating a Spice City sandwich to Lenovo giving thousands of employees a floating “gaming” holiday to Twitch building technology that helps advertisers avoid the game’s more mature scenes, marketers were eager to find their way in.

Craig Elimeliah is chief creative officer at Code and Theory.

Select ADWEEK as one of your favorite sources and stay up to date on our latest content.

Plus, why fragmentation is getting in the way of better consumer experiences.

From an official day off work from Logitech to the “Spice City” sandwich from Popeyes, brands are nudging their way into the conversation.

#7413. Skydance’s Rebecca Mall Announces New Marketing Leadership Team

Skydance’s marketing division is the latest unit to reveal its new leadership structure. Following its acquisition of Warner Bros. Discovery in October, Skydance has been busy working to mesh the operations of the two media companies.

Chief marketing officer Rebecca Mall, in a memo to her team, announced the new vision for the marketing team. “We have an incredible opportunity to build a more unified, innovative and effective marketing organization – one that breaks down silos, understands our audiences better than anyone and harnesses the power of creativity, data and technology to set a new standard for our industry,” according to the memo.

She added that the team’s job is not just to market what they create, but also to help drive growth and success for the entire Skydance company.

Mall revealed that cross-company marketing and corporate brand, data & analytics, media, partnerships, and franchise and kids & family will now be housed under one roof. The new leadership team includes Annah Zafrani, who will be tasked with leading Skydance’s cross-company marketing and brand unit. Zafrani, who comes from Paramount Skydance, will coordinate priority campaigns across the media company’s businesses and lead corporate brand marketing. Reporting to her will be, also from the Paramount Skydance side, Katie Hutchings, svp, brand marketing, creative and strategy, and her team.

Paramount Skydance’s Angie Sharma will lead franchise and kids & family, building one plan per franchise across content, consumer products, games, and experiences, with kids & family viewed as a key growth priority.

Jamal Salmon, who comes from Paramount Skydance, will lead data & analytics for marketing, and will also report to Josh Goldstine, president of worldwide marketing & distribution, who will also be responsible for film marketing. Salmon will also have a dotted line to Hugh Williams, chief engineer and evp for consumer and monetization engineering; and Josh Walker, chief financial and strategy officer for direct to consumer. The DTC unit oversees streaming and video-on-demand.

Paramount Skydance’s Michelle Hagen will lead brand partnerships, with Julie Moore, evp of brand partnerships, reporting to her, and Dana Nussbaum, co-head of global motion pictures marketing, Warner Bros., focusing on theatrical. Malene Sam, svp, originals, who was part of Paramount Skydance, report both to Hagen and Shauna Spenley, who leads DTC media. Under Spenley, Sam will be focused on TV and DTC.

Finally, Seth Goren, who comes from WBD, will lead media, bringing together media strategy, media buying, and agency management across the company.  He will report to Mall. But for theatrical media work, he also will report to Nussbaum. For his DTC media work, he will report to Spenley, and for his TV work, he will have a reporting line to Mike Benson, who leads TV Marketing.

The realignment of the marketing team has led to the departure of Meg Maro, the global head of franchise. Mall thanked her for her contributions during her time at the company.

Maro concluded saying: “We have an extraordinary opportunity to redefine what marketing can achieve in our industry, and I can’t wait to see what we accomplish together.”

#7409. Growth Copywriter at Winona

Winona is one of the leading telemedicine companies providing HRT for women in menopause. We’ve built all of our technology in house including an EHR, patient care admin system, patient portal, website, marketing technology etc. Winona has two compounding pharmacies and a team of in house physicians providing world-class care via a fully vertically integrated business model.

We are looking for a data-backed, empathetic Growth Copywriter to own the performance and optimization of our end-to-end messaging ecosystem. At Winona, copy isn’t just about sounding nice. It’s a measurable variable in our growth funnel. You will design and execute rapid copy experiments across our paid acquisition, intake flows, and lifecycle retention loops to help women confidently access bioidentical HRT and menopause care.

Full-Funnel Experimentation. Write copy hypotheses, design rapid A/B tests alongside growth and analytics teams, analyze performance data, and turn winning variations into a scalable messaging playbook.

Lifecycle Growth & Retention. Write, test, and optimize automated onboarding, activation, adherence, churn-save, and win-back sequences across email, SMS, and push notifications to drive LTV.

High-Intent Conversion Surfaces. Own the persuasive strategy and microcopy for landing pages, interactive quiz/intake flows, checkouts, and dynamic pricing/offer messaging.

Paid Acquisition at Scale. Generate volume-driven concepts, hooks, and static/video copy variants for Meta, Google, and YouTube, systematically iterating based on creative testing data.

Voice of Customer (VoC) Intelligence. Continuously mine product reviews, customer support tickets, intake data, and exit surveys to isolate deep qualitative insights and high-converting customer language.

AI-Augmented Workflows. Leverage and train Winona’s internal AI copywriting tools to scale production velocity, maintain brand consistency, and build automated variant pipelines without sacrificing quality.

5+ years of dedicated copywriting experience specializing in direct-response, conversion copywriting, or growth marketing.

DTC Telehealth, Wellness, or HealthTech experience is highly preferred. You understand how to write about sensitive health topics with clinical authority and deep empathy.

A portfolio backed by data and results — the exact copy variants you tested, the structural or psychological changes you made, and the quantifiable lift they produced in metrics like CTR, quiz completion, or conversion rates.

Deep lifecycle marketing mastery: significant experience building and optimizing automated email and SMS flows (onboarding, retention, win-backs), rather than just writing one-off promotional campaigns. You know how to nurture a lead over time.

A strong foundation in A/B testing mechanics — comfortable formulating clear copy hypotheses, understanding sample size requirements, and knowing when a test result has achieved true statistical significance.

The balance of empathy and compliance: a proven ability to write about highly sensitive personal health topics (menopause symptoms, hormonal changes, wellness) with profound empathy while strictly adhering to healthcare compliance rules and ad-network medical restrictions.

An experimenter’s ego — you don’t take copy critiques personally. You’re obsessed with letting consumer behavior and performance data crown the winning angle.

As a full-time member of our team, you’ll enjoy:

Paid non-working holidays per country of residence

Medical/ Dental/ Vision insurance (US-W2)

Employee engagement activities and virtual gatherings

#7405. Ticker: Willie Geist to Depart MS NOW, Committing to NBC News Full-time

Top of the Ticker: After a nearly 20-year run, Willie Geist will be departing MS NOW for an expanded role at NBC News. Geist will continue to focus his efforts on Sunday Morning and broaden his presence across Today and other NBC News platforms.

In announcing his departure on Monday, during Morning Joe, Geist said, “I am so grateful to both NBC and MSNOW for the unique opportunity to do what I love at both networks. He added, “This is a bittersweet goodbye to Joe, Mika, and the place that made possible everything else in my career over the last two decades, but I am thrilled to settle in with my family at Today and dedicate all of my time to that special place.”

Geist was the only remaining on-air talent still pulling double duty for MS NOW and NBC News after NBCUniversal spun off its cable network assets into a new media company, Versant, in 2025.

Continued independence: Skydance CEO David Ellison, during The New York Times’ DealBook Summit held on Wednesday, reiterated that its two news outlets, CBS News and CNN, will continue to operate as separate entities and be editorially independent. He did say there might be “some news sharing in the future,” but was happy with how they are currently positioned.

Distribution agreement: The free streaming Newsmax channel, Newsmax2, will now be available over the air across 112 television stations owned by HC2 Brodcasting. This widens Newsmax2’s footprint, as it will now be available in all of the top 40 U.S. television markets and 57 of the top 60.

Milestone unlocked: ABC News’ Sunday public affairs program This Week celebrated its 45th anniversary on Sunday, November 15. To toast the occasion ABC News hosted a party on November 12 at the Jefferson Hotel in Washington, D.C., which featured remarks from ABC News president Almin Karamehmedovic, anchor George Stephanopoulos, and co-anchors Martha Raddatz and Jonathan Karl.

Highest honor: Meanwhile, Martha Raddatz received the National Press Club’s Fourth Estate Award at a gala on Nov. 12 in Washington, D.C. Present to witness this occasion were Raddatz’s ABC News colleagues, including Mary Bruce, Rachel Scott, Mark Burstein, and daughter Greta Williams.

Paging Dr. Gupta: MS NOW now has its own doctor named Gupta, as the news network announced a new exclusive series for its Membership platform, On Call with Dr. Vin Gupta, where he will explain medical stories, conduct interviews, and take questions from MS NOW Members. Rival CNN has its own Dr. Gupta, of course, chief medical correspondent Sanjay Gupta.

#7397. Senior Forward Deployed Engineer - Toronto Remote at Camunda

Camunda is the enterprise platform for agentic orchestration, enabling organizations to coordinate AI agents, people, and systems across complex, end-to-end business processes. With built-in governance, auditability, and human oversight, Camunda gives enterprises the control they need to move AI from pilots to production — safely and at scale. Trusted by over 700 organizations worldwide, including 9 of top 10 US banks, Camunda helps enterprises boost operational efficiency, accelerate time-to-value, and deliver better customer experiences.Fully remote and global, we are in the middle of something bigger: transforming into an AI-first organisation, built on our own platform. We use Agentic AI to automate, orchestrate intelligent processes, and elevate human contribution across every team.Named GP Bullhound’s Top 100 Next Unicorn list, 2025 Great Place to Work certified. Visionary in 2025 Gartner® Magic Quadrant™ for Business Orchestration and Automation Technologies. ranked 3rd in Flexa's 2026 Most Flexible Companies, We’re growing fast and looking for top talent to join our team. If you want meaningful work, visible impact and put something genuinely rare on your CV, keep reading.

ProcessOS is a new kind of Camunda product: business processes described in natural language, generated as executable BPMN workflows, and improved autonomously from production data. We’re hiring the first cohort of Forward Deployed Engineers to bring it into production at enterprise customers. You’ll own the deployment journey from first process file to a working, improving system, and hand off a functioning capability to the customer’s team. The code you write and the patterns you discover feed directly back into how ProcessOS is built. This is a rare opportunity to shape a product, a function, and a field practice from day one.

You’ll work end‑to‑end across Java services and React interfaces, collaborating closely with a talented global team to measure, improve, and scale performance and fault tolerance in real-world distributed systems.

Deploy and prove. Own the journey from first process file to production workflow. At 90 days: at least one workflow running, the improvement loop active, and measurable fitness gains the customer can see. At 12 months: 3–5 customers with viable deployments.

Drive organizational adoption. Work across technical and business stakeholders to build understanding and trust, turning early deployments into lasting customer capability.

Work at depth technically. Configure and run Camunda clusters locally and in cloud environments. Debug integration failures. Build custom agents where platform defaults don’t fit. Write connector logic that gets legacy enterprise systems talking to ProcessOS.

Build the playbook. There is no deployment template yet. Document what good looks like as you discover it, and make the next implementation faster.

Feed the product. Custom agents you write get contributed back. Integration patterns get codified. Production bugs you find, you often fix. Your field work is a direct input to ProcessOS.

Hand off deliberately. Leave customers with a team that can operate independently, is trained on the platform, and acts on improvement results without you.

Enterprise production experience: you’ve shipped something inside a large organization and stayed to see it operate. You know why governance and resilience matter at scale.

Technical depth: You can debug an integration failure, write a custom agent against a legacy API, stand up a Camunda cluster from scratch, and explain generated BPMN to both engineers and business leads.

Stakeholder communication: you work effectively across engineering, architecture, and business leadership and adapt how you communicate without losing precision.

Practical AI experience: you’ve used AI to solve real problems in production – not just experimented. You understand context management, prompt engineering, agent testing, and integration tradeoffs.

Comfort with ambiguity: you make sound judgment calls without a playbook and document what you learn.

Experience in a customer-facing engineering or professional services role.

Familiarity with regulated industries such as financial services, healthcare, or insurance

Production experience with agentic systems or AI-integrated workflows.

Contributions to a shared platform or open source project that others built on.

#7392. Senior Forward Deployed Engineer at Clutch

Clutch builds AI agents that work on behalf of credit unions. Emma, our collections agent, is live at five credit unions and handled 60,000+ member conversations in her first twelve weeks at one of them. Behind her is a growing portfolio: origination, fraud step-up, member (re)engagement and activation agents, all built on the same Agents Platform (TypeScript, NestJS, our own agent SDK, MCP, Temporal, promptfoo).

As a FDE you are one of the two engineers on an agent. You take it from "sold" to "live and performing" at a specific credit union: you sit in the discovery sessions, you build the integrations to their core banking and loan systems, you author and tune the agent's behavior, you run the live tests with their staff, and you stay with it through go-live and the first weeks of production. When something needs a person in the room, you are the one who flies.

This is a senior engineering role, not a solutions or support role. You write production code in our monorepo every week: integrations, modules, and the agents themselves (prompts, tools, evals), together with the other engineers on the pod. The difference from a traditional engineer is that you are on site more often, you make judgment calls in front of the customer, and you own the outcome. What you learn in the field flows straight back into the product: defaults, playbooks, and the platform capabilities the next agent will need.

You build; our AI Operations Managers deliver. The pod (pod lead plus engineers) designs and builds each agent in a high-touch way with the first customers. Once the agent is defined and live, AI Ops coordinates the implementations, configuration and ongoing success at the next credit unions. You hand them something that works and a playbook to run it.

This role requires roughly 20% of travel on average, with one- to two-week stretches around kickoffs and go-lives. Based in the US, able to travel on short notice

You'll join the Agents team, a small, senior group operating as a startup inside Clutch: seven engineers, a dedicated AI product group, and AI sales specialists, working directly with some of the largest credit unions in the country. Every agent is staffed by a pair of engineers, one leaning toward the conversation and LLM side, one toward software and integrations, with a pod lead who owns the product day to day. There is no layer between you and the customer, and no layer between you and the platform team.

The operating style is direct, written, and evidence-driven: BLUF updates, evals over opinions, business outcomes over ticket counts. The team is spread across Brazil, Argentina and the US and works async by default. Being on site is a tool we use at specific moments (kickoff, live testing, go-live), not a permanent posting.

Ship your first production change to a live agent through the team's review process, and be able to explain how a member's call flows through the Agents Platform end to end

Learn one agent deeply: system prompt, tool contracts, orchestration triggers, compliance gates, and the implementation process from kickoff to go-live

Join at least one credit union implementation as the second engineer, including a live testing session with their staff

Map where the current implementation process loses time and where a default or a tool would have removed a round trip

Own the technical side of one credit union implementation end to end: core and LOS integration, agent configuration, testing, go-live, and the first weeks of production

Run the eval and tuning loop for your agent (golden transcripts, rubric judges, regression suite) and be trusted to change its behavior without a second engineer checking your work

Have completed at least two on-site visits and turned what you heard there into product decisions with your pod lead and PM

Contribute at least one reusable capability back to the Agents Platform that the next implementation will not have to rebuild

Have taken one net-new agent from sold to live at its first credit union, with data you can show the partner

Be the engineer credit union staff ask for by name when something in their deployment needs to change

Have written the implementation playbook for your agent well enough that the AI Ops team runs the second and third credit union without you

Be shaping what the next agents look like, because you have seen where the current ones break in the field

7+ years of software engineering in a fast-paced environment, preferably in SaaS and/or Fintech, with senior TypeScript and Node.js backend experience in a large codebase (NestJS, CQRS, schema-validated boundaries, dependency injection). We do not count years of AI experience; the field is too young for that to mean anything. We look at what you have shipped and how you reason about it

Integration and API engineering: secure service-to-service endpoints, third-party APIs, webhooks, and the patience to work with legacy banking systems that were not designed to be integrated

Security and auth judgment: OAuth and machine-to-machine auth, tenant-scoped access, secrets handling, no PII in logs. Our agents touch sensitive member data and operate under consumer-contact regulation

Customer presence: you can sit across from a VP of Collections, hear a request, and say "here is what I recommend and why", including when the answer is no. You are comfortable making calls in the room and owning them afterwards

Code-quality judgment under deadline pressure: you take on debt deliberately, name it, and pay it down, rather than gold-plating or leaving silent debt behind

Genuine appetite for LLM agents: eval discipline for non-deterministic systems, reading a system prompt like code, understanding that some decisions must never reach the model. Curiosity plus shipped work is enough; research depth is not required

Nice to have, explicitly not required: voice and telephony (LiveKit, Twilio), Temporal or event-driven orchestration, Python, core banking or LOS integration experience, credit union or banking domain knowledge

#7388. Staff GTM Systems Engineer at LiveKit

LiveKit is building the infrastructure layer for the voice-driven era of computing. Our platform gives developers everything they need to build, test, deploy, scale, and observe agents in production. Founded in 2021, LiveKit powers voice AI applications for OpenAI, xAI, Salesforce, Coursera, Spotify, and thousands of others, collectively facilitating billions of calls each year.

The Staff GTM Systems Engineer at LiveKit is the technical owner and architect of the systems that power our go-to-market engine. You'll decide how our GTM stack is designed, what we build versus buy, and where we invest next — and you'll be hands-on building it: Salesforce architecture, integrations, enrichment pipelines, and the agentic workflows that help Sales and Marketing move faster and smarter.

Salesforce is our system of record for revenue, pipeline, and customer data. HubSpot is our marketing layer for campaigns and lead generation, and what it captures needs to flow cleanly and reliably into Salesforce. Around them sits a growing stack of enrichment, sales intelligence, billing, and data platform tools. You'll own the architecture that ties it all together: disciplined change management, observable pipelines, data models built for AI consumption, and agentic workflows that act on GTM signals at scale — designed to serve the business for years, not quarters.

This role sits at the intersection of GTM, Data, Finance, and Engineering. You'll work directly with senior leaders to shape the GTM systems roadmap, turn business strategy into technical plans, and guide decisions that span multiple teams. You'll also help grow the engineers around you, setting the technical standards and practices the team builds on.

You understand the difference between owning a system and gatekeeping one. Sales lives in Salesforce. Marketing lives in HubSpot. Your job is to make sure both are governed well enough that others can work in them effectively, and powerful enough that the GTM team can do things they couldn't do before.

You'll thrive as a Staff GTM Systems Engineer if you:

Are known as the go-to person for tough technical problems, and have good judgment about which ones matter most.

Think in systems and long time horizons, and can make confident architectural decisions with incomplete information.

Can clearly explain complex technical concepts and tradeoffs to engineers and executives alike.

Believe systems should be managed with engineering discipline.

Ship fast and reliably — your work holds up in production.

Are a fast learner, frequently picking up new languages and tools.

Find real satisfaction in mentoring other engineers and helping them grow.

Own the architecture of the GTM systems stack: define how Salesforce, HubSpot, enrichment, sales intelligence, billing, and the data platform fit together, and write the technical strategy that guides how it evolves.

Lead build-vs-buy and procurement decisions: evaluate vendors, run proofs of concept, assess security and integration fit, and partner with Director of GTM Systems and Analytics, Finance, Legal, and IT on selection and renewals.

Shape the GTM systems roadmap with senior leaders across Sales, Marketing, RevOps, Finance, and Engineering: align on requirements, sequence investments, make clear tradeoffs, and lead cross-functional initiatives to completion.

Own Salesforce as the engineering system of record for revenue: data model, flows and automation, validation rules, field governance, and access control.

Own HubSpot for campaigns and lead generation, along with the HubSpot-Salesforce sync — so leads, contacts, and engagement signals reach the revenue pipeline without drift or silent failures.

Build and maintain integrations across the GTM stack, and partner with GTM Analytics Engineering on reliable data flows to the central data platform.

Design and deploy agentic workflows that consume GTM data, surface actionable signals, and automate manual operations — and set the patterns and guardrails others build on.

Establish observability and change management for critical GTM systems: alerts, dashboards, and runbooks; reviewed code changes, documented config, and rollback paths.

Mentor engineers across experience levels and set the technical standards for the team.

Partner with the Billing Systems Engineer to automate the CPQ-to-Billing handoff.

Extensive engineering experience in GTM or business systems, including owning a GTM stack's architecture end to end — build-vs-buy calls, vendor evaluations, and systems designed for a multi-year horizon.

Deep, hands-on Salesforce expertise — you understand the data model, have built complex flows and automation, and you think about it as an engineering system, not an admin tool. This is the primary bar.

HubSpot experience in a marketing context, including the HubSpot-Salesforce sync and what breaks and why.

Technical depth beyond the UI — you've built integrations with APIs, webhooks, and event streams, and you know when a native tool (a Flow, a Clay table, a HubSpot workflow) is the right answer versus when it's time to write code.

Agentic workflow experience — you've designed and deployed AI agents that act on GTM data in production.

Strong change management practices — you've shipped changes to live systems while protecting active deals and campaigns.

Experience influencing senior stakeholders — translating business strategy into system requirements and roadmaps.

A history of mentoring engineers, from early-career to senior.

Salesforce CPQ: approvals, order forms, discounting rules, and the closed-won-to-billing path.

SQL and data modeling fluency; familiarity with a warehouse-centric stack (BigQuery, Snowflake, dbt, reverse ETL).

Experience with billing platforms (Stripe, Zuora, Maxio) and the handoffs between GTM systems and billing.

Experience managing a GTM tooling budget or vendor portfolio.

Experience as a technical lead on a GTM or business systems team.

An opportunity to build something truly impactful to the world

#7384. The Art, Science and Theater of Modern Marketing ft. Thomas Ranese

What if the best marketing advantage you have is something that cannot be replicated by an algorithm?

For Thomas Ranese, CMO of Intuit, it starts with theater, empathy and understanding what people are actually trying to achieve.

In this episode of Marketing Vanguard, Jenny Rooney sits down with Thomas to unpack how an unconventional career spanning theater, Google and Uber shaped his approach to marketing leadership.

From growing up behind the counter of his father’s pasta shop to leading marketing at one of the world’s biggest financial technology platforms, Thomas gets into why emotional connection can be just as powerful as performance data.

He also addresses why AI needs human expertise to earn customer trust, and why brands should bring more humanity, creativity, and purpose into modern marketing.

Thomas Ranese is Chief Marketing Officer at Intuit, a global technology platform serving millions of customers worldwide with TurboTax, QuickBooks, Credit Karma and Mailchimp.

His career has taken him through some very different worlds, including theater, consulting, government and nonprofit work, Google and Uber. His approach to marketing is rooted in a simple idea: understand what people are trying to achieve, then build brands and experiences that help them get there. He strongly believes in storytelling and using humor to make complex information more relatable.

At Intuit, Thomas is helping shape the company’s evolution into an integrated, AI-powered financial technology platform, with a focus on combining technology with human expertise to help consumers and businesses prosper.

07:18 Marketing Is Theater, and It Goes Beyond Advertising — Thomas breaks down the three different kinds of theater that shape modern marketing: the storytelling and drama of advertising, the emotions and interactions built into customer experience, and the immersive experiences brands create through retail. He also believes real-world human interactions will become increasingly important as more of our lives move into digital and AI-powered environments.

12:44 What a Pasta Shop Taught Him About Customer Obsession — Thomas traces his interest in marketing back to his childhood, when he spent time working in his father’s small pasta shop. He loved being behind the counter, helping customers and figuring out what they needed. He even created his father’s first promotional newsletter on a Commodore 64. Looking back, he sees those early experiences as the foundation of his approach to customer connection, communication and service.

16:01 Why AI Needs Human Intelligence Too — Intuit is building AI into the core of its financial technology platform, but Thomas is particularly focused on the role human experts play alongside it. When customers are making important decisions about taxes, credit or their businesses, getting the right answer matters. AI can provide powerful technology and efficiency, but human expertise brings verification, empathy and the confidence customers need to act.

19:57 Money Is a Means to an End. So Is Your Product — Thomas challenges brands to think beyond what their products do and focus on what customers want to achieve with them. Small business owners aren’t necessarily starting businesses because they want to make money; they’re pursuing a craft, building a lifestyle, serving customers or creating opportunities for their families. The same principle applies across categories: brands become more meaningful when they understand the bigger ambitions their products help people achieve.

22:00 Intuit’s Goal for Financial Literacy — Thomas explains how Intuit is connecting its mission to power prosperity with financial literacy education. Through Intuit for Education, the company aims to reach 50 million students by 2030, addressing the fact that only 32% of students graduate able to pass a basic financial literacy exam. For Thomas, this is an opportunity to create meaningful social impact while building trust and introducing a new generation to the tools that can help them succeed.

26:02 Why CMOs Should be Fully Involved — Thomas pushes back on the idea that senior marketing leaders should step away from execution as their organizations grow. With technology and AI changing marketing so quickly, he believes CMOs need to stay hands-on, understand the tools and continue learning. He argues that the bigger the organization, the more important this becomes, especially when it is easy to become disconnected from the actual work and the customers being served.

Jenny Rooney is Chief Brand and Community Officer, leading strategy for the overall ADWEEK brand as well as the ways in which we serve and support our audiences with high-value content, products, partnerships and experiences, notably through our community programs such as Marketing Vanguard.

Select ADWEEK as one of your favorite sources and stay up to date on our latest content.

#7373. The Media RFP Is Quietly Becoming Obsolete ft. Bruce Williams

Media used to be one part of the marketing machine, but this approach became outdated a while ago.

In this episode of Marketing Vanguard, Jenny Rooney sits down with Bruce Williams, CEO of dentsu X North America, to examine how media is becoming the connective tissue across creative, commerce, CRM, data, and technology.

Bruce reveals why standalone media RFPs are disappearing, and what it really takes to design connected consumer experiences.

He also addresses why brands need to move from channel-first planning toward operating systems built around outcomes.

Bruce Williams is CEO of dentsu X North America. Here, his focus is increasingly on helping brands move beyond isolated media planning toward connected operating models that bring together data, content, technology and consumer experience.

With more than 20 years in media, Bruce has spent much of his career working at the intersection of media strategy, integrated marketing, technology and organizational transformation.

He also works with clients on AI-enabled transformation, agency interoperability and new ways to turn data into faster decision-making at both global and local levels.

01:26 The “Media RFP” Is Quietly Becoming Obsolete — Bruce says media RFPs rarely arrive as standalone asks anymore. Brands increasingly want integrated briefs that connect media with creative, experience, CRM and loyalty, or they want agencies to solve bigger transformation problems. So, the agency conversation has to shift from “Can you buy media well?” to “Can you help us operate better?”

03:13 Your Customer Doesn’t Experience Your Brand in Silos — Marketing may still be organized into separate teams, systems and channels, but the person receiving the experience does not care about those distinctions. Bruce argues that fragmentation remains one of the industry’s longest-running problems because disconnected structures eventually show up as disconnected consumer experiences.

06:22 On AI: Build the System, Then Stay Scrappy — Bruce does not see AI transformation as a choice between massive infrastructure projects and quick tactical wins. He points to work with Heineken that combines long-term data and technology integration with practical tools like local intelligence agents that move competitive and market data directly into local decisions.

08:51 Why Agencies Can’t Keep Saying “Our Way or Nothing” — For Bruce, the future of agency relationships comes down to interoperability and optionality. Closed systems create friction. Agencies need to work directly with brand data, technology and other partners instead of expecting clients to conform to one proprietary model. Openness is becoming part of the value proposition.

Jenny Rooney is Chief Brand and Community Officer, leading strategy for the overall ADWEEK brand as well as the ways in which we serve and support our audiences with high-value content, products, partnerships and experiences, notably through our community programs such as Marketing Vanguard.

#7332. Senior Analytics Engineer at Kit

Kit is the email marketing platform experts choose to grow their audience, deepen their relationships, and build a business around the work they love. For coaches, YouTubers, authors, podcasters, and other creatives, there isn't a better marketing hub to rely on to grow audiences, automate email marketing, and sell digital products — all within one platform.More importantly, there isn't a team more committed to helping creators earn a living. We're on a mission to help creators earn $1 billion using our creator marketing platform. We have always been 100% independent and 100% remote.We are proud to have built a product that our customers love, and we look for people who have enthusiasm and belief in our mission, vision, and values to join our team. We're also embracing AI thoughtfully — both in how we build and how we hire to ensure our team is adaptable, innovative, and ready for what's next.

Our Sales team needs a dedicated data partner, and creator commerce (how creators monetize by selling digital products) is becoming a bigger part of how Kit and our creators scale. Kit is growing, so we're looking for a Senior Analytics Engineer to own Kit's sales and commerce data from end to end.

Accurate sales data gives our team a clear picture of the pipeline and the confidence to make informed decisions. That’s what makes this role so important.

Your primary focus will be Sales: the dbt models that turn our CRM data into trusted pipeline, deal, and funnel metrics, and the Omni dashboards our sales leader and Senior Revenue Operations Manager use to run the business. You'll also model creator commerce data, so we understand what creators sell and earn through Kit, and you'll pick up reporting work with our Product team, such as feature usage reporting. On a small data team, we step outside our usual lanes when the need is there, so the mix will shift over time.

You'll build for two kinds of consumers: people and AI. Teammates increasingly answer their own questions by querying our semantic layer through Omni's AI and MCP connections to AI tools. That means your models, metric definitions, and documentation need to be clear and correct enough for an AI agent to use them without a data teammate in the loop.

This is a full-time individual contributor role for someone who likes being close to the business, enjoys turning messy operational data into numbers people trust, and is happy to work on both the models and the dashboards that sit on top of them. When Sales and Product have clear, reliable data, we help more creators build more valuable businesses with Kit.

You'll report to Sam Umachi, Head of Data (Linkedin).

Sales and RevOps are your primary partners, who will rely on the models and reporting you own. You'll also partner with our Product team on reporting for features like Visual Automations and on how creators use Kit.

Within our data team, you'll work alongside a Staff Analytics Engineer who owns org-wide semantic layer governance and modeling standards, a Senior Data Engineer who owns ingestion and pipelines, and a Senior Business Analytics Partner focused on revenue opportunities. This role has no direct reports.

First Week: Work through our onboarding curriculum in Notion and meet the data team, our Senior Revenue Operations Manager, the sales team, and your product partners. Get set up in our core tools: dbt Cloud, GitHub, Amazon Redshift, Omni, HubSpot, Linear, Hightouch and Slack. Set up your AI-assisted development environment and learn the shared AI workflows our data team uses. Open and merge your first small pull request so you learn our review and deployment flow early.

First Month: Map how HubSpot objects (deals, pipelines and stages, companies, contacts, and owners) flow into our dbt models, and document the gaps. Take over day-to-day ownership of the sales models and Omni dashboards that teammates have been covering. Audit current sales reporting and list where definitions differ or numbers don't reconcile. Get familiar with how commerce data (products creators sell and the revenue they earn through Kit) lands in the warehouse today. Agree with RevOps and Sales on the most important questions they need answered. Review teammates' pull requests and contribute to our shared SQL, dbt, and documentation standards. Share a written reflection with the team on what you've learned and where you'd focus first.

First Six Months: Ship and maintain well-tested, documented dbt models for core sales metrics, such as pipeline, deal velocity, stage conversion, win rates, and the sales-led funnel. Expose those metrics through the Omni semantic layer, working within the team's governance standards. Rebuild or refresh the core sales dashboards so pipeline reviews and forecasting conversations run on numbers that don't need manual reconciliation. Resolve competing sales metric definitions by writing down the options and tradeoffs, aligning with Sales and RevOps, and documenting the outcome. Model creator-level commerce data, such as products sold, revenue earned through Kit, and conversion, in partnership with data engineering. Write semantic layer descriptions, metric definitions, and documentation that let Omni's AI and MCP-connected AI tools answer routine sales questions correctly, and test them against real questions from Sales, RevOps and Product. Get Sales to the point where routine questions are answered through self-serve, whether in Omni dashboards, Omni's AI, or AI tools connected over MCP, rather than one-off requests.

First Year: Be the recognized owner of Kit's sales data, the person Sales and RevOps go to first and trust without second-guessing. Give our revenue analysis work the sales and commerce models it depends on, so deeper questions about pipeline health and creator earnings can be answered quickly, thereby enabling us to make creators more successful. Leave your domains well documented and well tested, legible to new teammates, stakeholders, and the AI agents that query our semantic layer. Help raise the team's modeling quality through code review, pairing, and sharing what you learn.

Deep dbt experience. You build modular, tested, documented dbt projects, and you're comfortable with incremental models, snapshots, macros, CI, and scheduled jobs in dbt Cloud or similar.

Advanced SQL. You reason carefully about grain, joins, and window functions, and you write queries that are readable and performant on a cloud warehouse.

Data modeling for sales and GTM. You can model deals, pipeline stages and their history, ownership changes, and funnel conversion into clean, reusable structures. You are opinionated on Kimball standards and when a concept should be a fact or dimension.

CRM data fluency. You know how sales data behaves in a CRM, including stage changes, backdated edits, and custom properties. HubSpot is preferred, but other CRM experience (e.g. Salesforce) applies just as well. We care most that you recognize common CRM patterns.

AI-assisted development. You use AI code editors and agents (such as Cursor, Claude Code, Codex, or similar) every day, and you validate their output with tests, query results, and review before anything ships.

Clear written communication. You explain metric definitions, tradeoffs, and data caveats in plain language that Sales, RevOps, and Product partners understand.

BI and semantic layer experience. You can build dashboards and semantic models in Omni, Looker, or a similar BI tool, and you design metric definitions that people and AI tools can both query correctly.

Git-based workflows. You keep pull requests small and well described, and you give thoughtful code review.

Nice to have: Redshift performance tuning (sort and distribution keys), Python for scripting or API pulls, reverse ETL, and experience with product event, billing, payments, or ecommerce data.

Around 7 or more years in analytics engineering or data engineering where you personally shipped production data models.

Several years of hands-on work building and maintaining production dbt projects.

Working directly with sales teams and RevOps, and owning reporting they relied on to run the business.

Owning a data domain over time and evolving its models as the business and its questions changed.

Working at a SaaS company, ideally one with both a self-serve and a sales-led motion. Commerce, payments, or creator economy experience is a plus, not a requirement.

Working on a small or remote team where you were trusted to make decisions independently.

Ownership and follow-through. You treat your data domains as yours. You notice problems before stakeholders do, close the loop on requests, and finish what you start.

Judgment. You match rigor to the decision at hand, know when a quick answer is enough and when a request should become a durable model, and say so clearly.

Flexibility. You're glad to step outside your primary area when another team needs help, and you balance that work against your core commitments openly.

Teach everything you know. You document as you go, pair generously, and help stakeholders understand the data well enough to use it on their own.

Work in public. You share progress, open questions, and decisions in writing where the team can see them, including what didn't work.

Creator success is our success. You remember who's on the other end. A wrong number in a pipeline review means Sales spends time on the wrong creators, and that shapes how carefully you work.

Do it with excellence. You test your work, review it carefully, and don't ship a number you can't defend.

Candor with care. You're comfortable pushing back when a request won't answer the real question, and you do it in a way that builds trust.

Needing detailed specs or a highly structured environment before you can start.

Discomfort with ambiguity. Some of our sales and commerce definitions are still evolving, and you'll help settle them.

Needing several layers of approval to make modeling decisions within your domain.

Wanting to work only on dashboards, or only on pipelines, rather than across the transformation and BI layers.

Wanting a strictly fixed scope. Sales comes first, but you'll pick up Product or other work when the business needs it.

Wanting mostly greenfield work. Much of this role is improving sales models and dashboards that already exist.

Preferring to take tickets as written rather than partnering with stakeholders on what they need.

Being skeptical of AI's capabilities, or using AI without good judgment and accountability.

Expecting a large data team with dedicated specialists for every part of the stack.

#7323. Teacher prep programs probed in Education Department’s latest anti-DEI push

Let Higher Ed Dive's free newsletter keep you informed, straight from your inbox.

The agency said diversification efforts disregard “merit,” but critics say the programs level the playing field for historically excluded candidates.

The U.S. Department of Education opened a string of investigations into teacher preparation programs on Nov. 13, alleging that they discriminate on the basis of race under Title VI or sex under Title IX.

The investigations mark one of the first times the department's Office for Civil Rights has used civil rights protections to challenge diversification efforts in teacher scholarships, hiring, training and recruitment practices.

The programs at the center of the investigations are aimed at recruiting historically underrepresented groups in the teaching profession, such as men and teachers of color.

“Race, national origin, or sex should not decide who will shape America’s next generation, yet many states and universities seem to prioritize recruiting and training ‘minority’ candidates rather than evaluating candidates based solely on merit,” said Assistant Secretary for Civil Rights Kimberly Richey in a Nov. 13 statement.

“Education agencies and institutions that consider race, national origin, or sex as a means to withhold opportunities in selecting, supporting, and preparing teaching candidates violate federal law and compromise education excellence."

Richey concluded that students should be "judged on their hard work and merit, not prejudged by the color of their skin or their sex.”

EdTrust, a nonprofit that advocates for racial and economic equity in American education, said in a statement to K-12 Dive that the Trump administration is using civil rights protections meant to "dismantle discrimination and expand opportunity" to instead "undermine pathways into teaching for people historically excluded from the profession betrays that purpose."

“The administration’s false divide between ‘merit’ and diversity perpetuates the damaging suggestion that teachers of color are less qualified or less deserving," said Eric Duncan, director of Pre-K-12 policy of EdTrust, in the Nov. 16 statement. "It discounts their skills, expertise, and contributions while ignoring the systemic barriers that have denied talented people opportunities for generations. Scholarships that address those barriers help ensure that family wealth does not determine who can become a teacher."

The Education Department's investigations, Duncan said, "risk turning civil rights enforcement into a tool for preserving the very inequities it should confront.”

CUNY, which houses the NYC Men Teach Program being investigated by the department, said in a statement to K-12 Dive that it was "proud of its role educating the teachers of tomorrow and our programs are open to all students regardless of race, gender or national origin."

According to the city's website, the program was created around 2015 in collaboration with the federal Education Department and then-Mayor Bill de Blasio with the goal of putting an additional 1,000 men of color into the city’s teacher pipeline.

A March 2026 evaluation of the program conducted for the NYC mayor's office by Westat, which provides research services for government agencies, shows that the program contributed over 3,100 new male teachers of color to New York City Public Schools between school years 2015–16 and 2023–24.

The American Association of Colleges for Teacher Education said in a Nov. 16 email to K-12 Dive, “Strengthening and diversifying the educator pipeline is essential to addressing persistent teacher shortages and ensuring every PK-12 student has access to well-prepared, effective teachers. Decades of research confirm that a diverse educator workforce benefits all students academically, socially, and emotionally." AACTE added that it does not comment on active federal inquiries involving specific institutions or state agencies.

While the investigations are some of the first of their kind by OCR, they build on previous efforts by the Trump administration to weed out DEI in teacher preparation programs — especially in the wake of the U.S. Supreme Court’s 2023 ruling in Students for Fair Admissions v. Harvard.

Some of the earliest federal grants terminated in 2025 by the administration were under the Supporting Effective Educator Development Grant Program, the Teacher Quality Partnership Program and the Teacher and School Leader Incentive Program, due to what it said was the promotion of "divisive ideologies". The department admitted in a March court filing that it had cancelled 104 out of 109 active TQP and SEED grants.

The loss of over 100 federal teacher training grants last year caused at least 127 teacher candidates to drop out of their programs and another 259 accepted candidates to not enroll after all, according to survey findings released in October.

Those cuts were preceded by an anti-DEI directive that has since been heavily litigated and was most recently blocked in court in September.

That anti-DEI letter specifically called for agency personnel to review all new grant awards and issued grants to ensure those federal dollars “do not fund discriminatory practices — including in the form of DEI.” The directive added that “Illegal DEI policies and practices can violate both the letter and purpose of Federal civil rights law and conflict with the Department’s policy of prioritizing merit, fairness, and excellence in education.”

The Justice Department in August 2025 also warned districts they could be exposed to legal liability by asking job applicants how their “cultural background informs their teaching,” using recruitment strategies targeting candidates from specific geographic areas or racial backgrounds, and asking job candidates to describe how they overcame obstacles.

Since the Supreme Court’s decision in Students for Fair Admissions v. Harvard, civil rights groups have raised concerns that it would set back educational equity efforts even outside of race-conscious admissions. That decision undid race-conscious policies in college admissions, and the Education Department under Trump has used it to justify scaling back other diversity, equity and inclusion initiatives in education.

Scholarship availability, teacher pipelines and student affinity groups were among the top areas beyond college access that advocates were concerned could be impacted in the wake of the ruling.

#7313. DHS’ 4-year cap on student visa stays struck down by federal judge

Let Higher Ed Dive's free newsletter keep you informed, straight from your inbox.

The law does not grant the agency “such broad and open-ended authority to undermine” the student visa program, the judge wrote.

DHS published the rule in July, arguing it was needed to protect national security and prevent visa fraud. International students would have been able to seek extensions on their visas, but the decision would have been at federal officials’ sole discretion.

The rule would have changed long-standing policy permitting foreign students to stay in the U.S. for as long as it takes to complete their academic studies.

Several higher ed groups and unions, including the Presidents’ Alliance on Higher Education and Immigration and the American Federation of Teachers, sued the agency in August over the regulations.

Saylor handed the plaintiffs an early win when he temporarily blocked the visa cap from taking effect. Writing that they were likely to succeed in their case, the judge flagged that the rule could be abused by the Trump administration.

“It is not difficult to imagine how that system might be abused to punish those whose viewpoints or research are disfavored by the officials making the decision — or, indeed, punish disfavored institutions,” Saylor said in his Sept. 14 order.

He repeated those concerns Monday, describing the student visa process DHS sought to set up as an “unreviewable, completely discretionary renewal system.”

Saylor further said DHS failed to weigh “less burdensome” options.

The DHS rule would have done more than limit student visa stays. Some international students would have been barred from changing their field of study or transferring colleges. And they could not come to the U.S. to pursue an academic degree at the same or a lower level than a degree they already possess.

For example, an international student who holds a law degree would effectively be banned from pursuing a master’s degree in the U.S., Saylor said.

“Put bluntly, DHS is seeking to prevent or discourage the very activity — providing educational opportunities for foreign students in the United States — that Congress sought to permit and encourage,” he said. “The statute simply does not grant DHS such broad and open-ended authority” to undermine the student visa program.

The judge’s decision may not put the case to bed, as the Trump administration could still appeal it. The continued uncertainty comes as new data shows a decline in international enrollment in the U.S.

In 2025-26, the number of foreign students attending U.S. colleges dropped 2.7% year over year, according to the latest Open Doors report. And preliminary data from fall 2026 found that international enrollment declined another 9% annually at surveyed institutions. Both decreases were driven by losses of new students and those pursuing graduate degrees.

Colleges would be on the hook for up to $100,000 in fees for each international student seeking to participate in Optional Practical Training.

Analysts pointed to worse-than-expected fiscal 2026 financials and a projected budget shortfall for the current fiscal year.

#7311. Product Manager - Postgres at Supabase

Supabase is the Postgres development platform, built by developers for developers. We provide a complete backend solution including Database, Auth, Storage, Edge Functions, Realtime, and Vector Search. All services are deeply integrated and designed for growth.

We're hiring a Product Manager to own Postgres itself at Supabase. Every Supabase project is a full Postgres database, and Postgres is the foundation that Auth, Storage, Realtime and Edge Functions run on. You'll own the product experience of the database: Postgres versions and upgrades, maintenance, observability, extensions and security.

You'll work closely with our Postgres engineering teams and with the PM lead for Databases, who leads Multigres. You'll define the Postgres requirements that architecture must meet, and own how customers experience their database through that transition.

This role is ideal for someone who loves Postgres, sweats the details that make a database trustworthy, and is excited about improving the experience for millions of developers. Small improvements here compound across every project on the platform.

You'll own Postgres versions, upgrades and end-of-life. Which major and minor versions we support, how and when customers upgrade, and how we retire old versions. Your goal is to make upgrades routine rather than risky.

You'll make maintenance predictable. You'll design how we schedule, communicate and run maintenance windows so customers stay informed and in control, and disruption is rare and expected.

You'll help developers understand their database. Query performance, metrics, logs and advisors: you'll own the path from "my database is slow" to the fix.

You'll own our extension ecosystem. Which extensions we offer, how we evaluate and support new ones, and how we keep them working across versions.

You'll own Postgres security. Roles and permissions, Row Level Security ergonomics, secure defaults, and how we respond to Postgres and extension CVEs.

You'll talk to developers across the full spectrum, from indie developers on their first project to enterprises running critical workloads, and bring what you learn into every prioritization call.

You'll own the problem definition, requirements and success metrics for what we ship, such as upgrade success rate, the share of the fleet on supported versions, and time-to-diagnose for performance problems. You'll set the metric before the team builds, then track it after launch.

You'll keep Engineering, Support and GTM aligned. You'll own the plan of record for what's coming, why, and in what order, and communicate version changes and deprecations clearly to customers.

4+ years of Product Management experience on databases, developer tools or infrastructure, or equivalent depth as an engineer, DBA or founder moving into product. You're comfortable commenting on an architecture doc and advocating for the user when trade-offs are required.

Know Postgres well, or another relational database well enough to ramp quickly. You're comfortable reading EXPLAIN output and talking about replication, WAL, roles and extensions.

Have shipped products where reliability and trust are the experience. You treat downtime and breaking changes as product problems, not just operational ones.

Write clearly. Your specs, announcements and deprecation notices leave little room for confusion.

Build with what you own. You run queries, test upgrades and try extensions yourself, and form opinions through first-hand use.

Use AI to move faster. You use AI tools to compress research, drafting and feedback synthesis so you spend more time on judgment calls.

Work async by default. Supabase is fully remote. You make decisions in writing and move work forward without meetings.

Ran Postgres in production: upgrades, tuning, incident response.

Worked on database observability, performance tooling, or security and compliance products.

Contributed to Postgres, its extensions, or the wider open-source community.

We are looking for a Product Builder to join the IDP & RPA pod. You will take real problems from customers, decide what is worth solving, and build and ship the solution yourself, using AI tools to get from idea to production quickly without lowering the bar.

Product Building is how Camunda's Experience organization builds the applications people use on top of Camunda 8, such as Hub, Operate, and ProcessOS. Small pods of builders own a product area from customer problem to shipped change, with no hand-offs between product management, design, and engineering. We are setting this model up now, so you will help shape how it works.

The IDP & RPA pod builds how Camunda processes work with documents and legacy applications. With intelligent document processing (IDP), customers create extraction and classification templates in Camunda Hub and publish them so a process can use AI to turn invoices and other documents into structured data. With robotic process automation (RPA), customers integrate legacy applications that have no APIs, using Robot Framework scripts on a standalone RPA worker. The hard problems come with putting LLMs into customer processes: extraction quality, model and prompt choices, testing and trust, and working for AI agents as well as for people.

Help shape product direction for IDP & RPA by combining customer input, usage data, and field feedback into clear priorities.

Partner with the other builders and product strategy to turn problems into shipped features, using short written proposals and prototypes to align early.

Make judgment calls with incomplete information within your scope and own the outcome.

Communicate trade-offs and decisions clearly to stakeholders who don't live in the details the way you do.

Use AI tools across the whole loop, from specs to prototypes to production-ready code, and hold a high bar for what you ship by reviewing and improving the output rather than accepting it.

Build, test, and ship improvements to the IDP & RPA experience, reading errors and tracing issues far enough to give your pod useful context.

Talk to customers and to support and customer success colleagues about how IDP is used and where it breaks, and turn what you learn into decisions about what to build next.

Pick up customer incidents and trace them across Hub, the connectors, and the deployed process of the customer.

Track what you ship against real outcomes and adjust when the data tells you to.

#7295. ABA accreditation arm moves to nix full-time faculty standard for first-year classes

Let Higher Ed Dive's free newsletter keep you informed, straight from your inbox.

The U.S. Department of Justice recently threatened an antitrust probe over the requirement, arguing that it drives up legal education costs.

The ABA’s accreditation council voted to eliminate the standard while under pressure from the Justice Department. In an October letter, Associate Attorney General Stanley Woodward said the group could face an antitrust investigation if it didn’t eliminate the full-time faculty requirement, according to The Wall Street Journal.

Woodward argued that adjunct faculty are less expensive to hire and often have more firsthand experience, and that limiting their role in first-year law school education drives up costs and hurts students, the Journal reported.

The accreditation council also plans to seek public comment on further potential changes to its policies regarding full-time instructors that are meant to provide more flexibility to law schools.

The council is in communication with the Justice Department, a spokesperson for the council said Tuesday. The department did not immediately respond to a request for comment Tuesday.

Woodward’s letter came after the ABA’s accreditation council began seeking public comment in September on proposed changes to its full-time faculty requirements.

The accreditation council’s proposals drew mixed reactions from law schools and professors.

In a public comment, the faculty leadership of the University of Colorado Boulder’s law school opposed nixing the requirement regarding the first year of curriculum.

They argued that the first-year of law school “is a key period for educating students on core legal principles, doctrines, and analytical methods” and “a critically important time for creating community among law school students.”

“Full-time faculty are simply more available for answering student questions outside of class, as it is part of their full-time job to do so,” the faculty leaders wrote. “And perhaps most importantly, full-time faculty build pedagogical expertise in designing courses, sequencing learning, assessing progress, and giving constructive feedback.”

Yet others voiced support for the change. Terence Lau, dean of Syracuse University’s law school, argued that the changes would reduce “undue burden” while still preserving full-time faculty members’ “core role” in legal education.

Lau argued the current standard limits the ability for law schools to tap sitting judges or emeritus professors for first-year instruction. “Law schools should be able to draw on these teachers where they best serve students,” he wrote.

Last week’s vote comes as the accreditation council is under the microscope by the Trump administration.

In August, a report from U.S. Department of Education staffers recommended that the agency strip the ABA’s accreditation council of federal recognition. The staffers concluded that the council wasn’t compliant with over two dozen federal criteria and alleged that it wasn’t sufficiently independent from the ABA’s professional organization.

Losing federal recognition would mean the ABA’s accreditation council could no longer serve as a gatekeeper to Title IV financial aid. Although the group accredits some 200 law schools, only five rely on it for access to federal aid, a council representative said in September to a federal advisory panel reviewing the department’s findings.

That panel, which advises the Education Department on accreditation matters, deadlocked on a vote on whether to recommend that the council lose recognition. Education Under Secretary Nicholas Kent is soon expected to make a final call on the matter.

The ABA’s accreditation council has also caught flak from the Trump administration over its prior diversity standard. The council formally repealed the standard in September after suspending it in February 2025.

#7293. Information Security Engineer - Application Security at Camunda

Camunda is the enterprise platform for agentic orchestration, enabling organizations to coordinate AI agents, people, and systems across complex, end-to-end business processes. Hi there! We're hiring an Information Security Engineer with a focus on Application Security to join our Information Security team at Camunda.

We are a team that takes security seriously without making it feel like a locked door. We work in a transparent, straightforward way, and we care about doing the work right rather than just checking a compliance box.

You will work side by side with our R&D engineers: reviewing designs, threat modeling new features, digging into code and helping teams make good, risk-based decisions about the vulnerabilities that come in. You'll also help build the internal tools that make this work scale, including our CVE registry and the LLM-assisted workflows we use to analyse code and triage findings.

Camunda is an AI-first company, and InfoSec is no exception: AI agents are part of how we review code, assess vulnerabilities, and build our tools. You will help shape how that works.

This is a hands-on role for someone who already has solid AppSec foundations and wants to grow. We are a generalist team, so you will also take part in our on-call rotation and incident response, and get exposure to other areas of security. But most of your time will be spent with engineers and building and maintaining internal tools and processes.

Vulnerability management: triage findings from SCA, container and code scanning, external reports and internal security testing; assess whether Camunda is actually affected; agree fix / mitigate / accept with engineering and follow through to release.

Threat modeling and security reviews: run threat modeling sessions and design reviews with R&D teams for new features and architecture changes, and turn the results into concrete, prioritised actions.

Code analysis with LLMs: use and improve LLM-assisted tooling (agents, prompts, reusable skills) to review code, validate findings and reproduce issues, while applying your own judgement to know when the model is wrong.

Build internal security tooling: contribute to the CVE registry and related automation that connects scanners, engineering teams, and customer communication. Much of it is built on Camunda itself.

Help engineers do the secure thing by default: answer questions, review pull requests, improve guidelines and occasionally run a session or workshop.

On-call and incident response: share the InfoSec on-call rotation with the other engineers and help investigate and resolve security incidents.

Support the wider team: help write security advisories for our customers, answer customer security questions and support audits.

Around 3+ years in security or software engineering, with at least 2 of them working on application or product security (AppSec, product security, pentesting, or a security-focused developer role all count).

Experience at a SaaS or software product company, working closely with engineering teams that ship often through modern CI/CD and cloud environments.

Hands-on vulnerability triage: you've worked with tools like Snyk, Trivy, Semgrep, CodeQL or similar, can tell a real issue from a false positive, and can explain how exploitable something actually is in context (CVSS, exploitability, configuration).

Threat modeling and design review experience (e.g. STRIDE) on real systems, ideally web apps, APIs, or distributed services.

You read and write code. You're comfortable reading Java (Camunda is built on it) well enough to follow a data flow and spot a security bug. You can write scripts and small tools and contribute to an existing codebase with review.

You build with AI, not just chat with it. You use AI coding agents (Claude Code, Cursor, Copilot or similar) in your daily work to write tools, analyse code or automate tasks. And you know their output still needs a second look, especially when it comes to security.

Clear, friendly communication with engineers. You see yourself as an enabler, not a gatekeeper, and you can explain risk to people who aren't security specialists.

Some Python and/or JavaScript experience, or experience building internal tools.

Experience responding to security incidents or being part of an on-call rotation.

Exposure to security advisories, CVE disclosure or bug bounty programmes.

Experience securing Kubernetes or container-based workloads, or open-source / B2B software.
